Well, I’m here because... honestly, it's work. Ever since this new operations manager started picking apart the inspection procedures I’ve used for years... I mean, I’ve been weighing and sampling on that line for so long, I could do it in my sleep. We used to have this manager, Dave, who was just wonderful. He actually just retired to Florida and bought one of those huge Airstream trailers. My husband and I were looking at RVs a few years ago, but the maintenance is just a nightmare, you know? 

But anyway, with this new guy, I just can’t seem to leave work at work. I come home so wound up, and I end up snapping at my husband over nothing. Losing interest? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. I still want to do my regular things and be with him. Like, I still really enjoy going to estate sales with him on Saturday mornings. The jars they sell are practically the size of a bucket, and I hate wasting food. But... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, feeling down. No, not down. Just... really on edge about the new manager.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that comes on very quickly, like your heart racing, feeling short of breath, or thinking you might be losing control?\n\nPatient: Intense fear? Oh, no, nothing like that. My heart doesn't suddenly race or anything. I mean, my husband did get me one of those smartwatches for my birthday to keep track of my steps and my heart rate, so I'd know if it was doing something weird. It has this really pretty rose gold band, which I love, but the charger is just ridiculous. It's this tiny little magnetic thing, and if you bump the nightstand even a fraction of an inch, it completely disconnects and stops charging. \n\nOur cat, Barnaby—he's a Maine Coon mix, just a massive ball of fur—he's always jumping up there in the middle of the night and knocking things over. He actually knocked my glasses off a few weeks ago and bent the arm so badly I had to take them in to get fixed. The girl at the vision center was telling me I should really put them in a hard case at night, but those cases are just so bulky, you know? They take up half the space in my purse, and I like to carry a smaller crossbody bag so my shoulder doesn't ache. I found this gorgeous tooled leather one at a craft fair up in... wait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, losing control or my heart racing. No, no, I don't get anything sudden like that. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Oh, constantly. I just can't seem to leave work at work anymore. It's really why I'm here, honestly. Ever since this new operations manager started picking apart the inspection procedures I’ve used for years... I mean, I've been doing these quality checks the exact same way for a decade, and now suddenly it's all wrong? He wants everything logged on these new tablets, and the warehouse Wi-Fi is just terrible. 

Speaking of Wi-Fi, my husband actually just tried to set up one of those mesh networks at our house because our router in the living room wasn't reaching the back patio. We like to sit out there in the evenings now that we got those new Adirondack chairs. We found them at an end-of-summer clearance sale at Lowe's—marked down from a hundred and fifty dollars to forty-five! The cashier even gave us an extra ten percent off because one had a tiny scratch on the armrest. I just took a brown Sharpie to it and you can't even tell. My sister thought I paid full price for them. 

But... wait, where was I going with that? Oh, right, being at home. Yeah, I come home so wound up from dealing with that manager that I end up snapping at my husband over absolutely nothing. And then when we go to bed, I just lie awake replaying all those meetings in my head. We even bought a new memory foam mattress to help us sleep better, but it sleeps so hot I had to buy special cooling sheets. They feel kind of slippery though, almost like sleeping on a parachute, I don't know if I like them. But even with the new bed, my brain just won't shut off. And then the next day I go into the plant so tired and stressed that I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all.

It has this really pretty rose gold band, which I love, but the charger is just ridiculous. It's this tiny little magnetic thing, and if you bump the nightstand even a fraction of an inch, it completely disconnects and stops charging. \n\nOur cat, Barnaby—he's a Maine Coon mix, just a massive ball of fur—he's always jumping up there in the middle of the night and knocking things over. He actually knocked my glasses off a few weeks ago and bent the arm so badly I had to take them in to get fixed. I found this gorgeous tooled leather one at a craft fair up in... wait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, losing control or my heart racing. No, no, I don't get anything sudden like that. We like to sit out there in the evenings now that we got those new Adirondack chairs. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
About two months now. It all really started when that new operations manager came in and decided my inspection procedures weren't good enough anymore. I mean, two months of this. 

You know, it's funny, two months ago was exactly when we decided to finally clean out the gutters. My husband had been putting it off since last fall, and the oak tree in our front yard just dumps leaves everywhere. We actually had to hire a guy to come do it because the ladder we have is one of those heavy aluminum extension ones, and it's just too awkward for us to maneuver around the landscaping. We planted these beautiful hydrangea bushes last spring—the Endless Summer variety, the ones that are supposed to bloom blue if you have acidic soil? Well, mine keep turning pink, and I read online that you can add coffee grounds to the dirt to change the pH. I started saving all the grounds from my morning pot of Folgers, which I've been drinking for years, even though my daughter keeps trying to get me to buy those fancy espresso pods. She has one of those machines that foams the milk for you, but honestly, who has time to clean all those little tubes every morning? It just seems like a breeding ground for bacteria if you ask me. 

Wait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, about two months. Ever since he started messing with how I do my job, I just can't seem to wind down at all.

He wants everything logged on these new tablets, and the warehouse Wi-Fi is just terrible. \n\nSpeaking of Wi-Fi, my husband actually just tried to set up one of those mesh networks at our house because our router in the living room wasn't reaching the back patio. We like to sit out there in the evenings now that we got those new Adirondack chairs. We found them at an end-of-summer clearance sale at Lowe's—marked down from a hundred and fifty dollars to forty-five! The cashier even gave us an extra ten percent off because one had a tiny scratch on the armrest. I just took a brown Sharpie to it and you can't even tell. My sister thought I paid full price for them. \n\nBut... wait, where was I going with that? Oh, right, being at home. Yeah, I come home so wound up from dealing with that manager that I end up snapping at my husband over absolutely nothing. And then when we go to bed, I just lie awake replaying all those meetings in my head. We even bought a new memory foam mattress to help us sleep better, but it sleeps so hot I had to buy special cooling sheets. They feel kind of slippery though, almost like sleeping on a parachute, I don't know if I like them. But even with the new bed, my brain just won't shut off. And then the next day I go into the plant so tired and stressed that I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about work and having trouble winding down at night?\n\nPatient: About two months now. It all really started when that new operations manager came in and decided my inspection procedures weren't good enough anymore. I mean, two months of this. \n\nYou know, it's funny, two months ago was exactly when we decided to finally clean out the gutters. My husband had been putting it off since last fall, and the oak tree in our front yard just dumps leaves everywhere. We actually had to hire a guy to come do it because the ladder we have is one of those heavy aluminum extension ones, and it's just too awkward for us to maneuver around the landscaping. We planted these beautiful hydrangea bushes last spring—the Endless Summer variety, the ones that are supposed to bloom blue if you have acidic soil? Well, mine keep turning pink, and I read online that you can add coffee grounds to the dirt to change the pH. I started saving all the grounds from my morning pot of Folgers, which I've been drinking for years, even though my daughter keeps trying to get me to buy those fancy espresso pods. She has one of those machines that foams the milk for you, but honestly, who has time to clean all those little tubes every morning? It just seems like a breeding ground for bacteria if you ask me. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, about two months. Ever since he started messing with how I do my job, I just can't seem to wind down at all.\n\n[Last Question]\nHave you found yourself avoiding certain tasks or responsibilities at work because they make you feel overly anxious or stressed since the new manager started?\n\nRules:\n- Return exactly ONE item. Well, yes, I suppose I have. My work record has really taken a hit lately because I’ve been missing internal deadlines, which is so unlike me. And I’ve definitely pulled back from the weekly meetings I’m expected to attend. I just can't stomach sitting in that conference room with him right now.

The waiter told us the secret was using real stone-ground grits and a ton of sharp cheddar, which I actually tried to recreate at home for my husband, but it just wasn't the same. The cheese got all clumpy and stuck to the bottom of my good non-stick pan, the one my daughter got me for Mother's Day... 

Wait, I'm sorry, my mind is just everywhere today. What were you asking? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yes, the meetings and the deadlines. And it's not just work that's falling apart, honestly. At home, my husband and I are arguing so much more lately over absolutely nothing, and I’m just not keeping up with our usual evening routines the way I used to. We used to always walk the neighborhood or play a card game after dinner, but now I just can't seem to manage it.

Well, I don't really know if I'd say I try to control it... I mean, I try to distract myself, sure. Like, I'll turn on the TV, or I'll pick up a book. I've been trying to read that new historical fiction novel everyone at my book club is talking about. It's set in World War II, in London, during the Blitz. The author apparently spent three years researching the subway stations they used as bomb shelters. It's fascinating, really, the way people just adapted to living underground like that. They even had little canteens set up, serving tea and sandwiches while bombs were literally dropping above them. 

My aunt actually lived in London during the war, though she was just a little girl then. She used to tell me stories about the rationing, how they'd save up their sugar rations for weeks just to bake a tiny cake for someone's birthday. I tried baking a cake from scratch last weekend, actually, thinking it would take my mind off things. A lemon drizzle cake, my favorite. But I forgot to check if my baking powder was expired, and the whole thing came out flat as a pancake. My husband still ate it, bless him, but it was just so dense...

I'm sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Rambling. What did you ask me? Oh, controlling the worry. Yeah, I don't know. I try to read or bake, but the thoughts just keep creeping back in, no matter what I'm doing. It's like my brain just won't let it go.

No, I wouldn't say I feel physically restless or on edge like that. I'm just... tired, mostly. Though speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking at all hours of the night lately, and that certainly doesn't help anyone settle down. Fatigued? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, like I said, but it's not a lack of energy every single day. It's more just... I don't know, mental exhaustion from all the worry. 

Speaking of energy, though, my daughter started taking this new supplement she read about online. Maca root, I think it's called? She swears it gives her the energy of a teenager again. She buys it in this powder form and mixes it into her morning smoothies. She's always been so health-conscious, ever since she did that study abroad program in California during college. She came back practically a vegan and completely obsessed with farmers' markets. We used to go to this wonderful market on Saturdays when she lived closer, they had this one stall that sold the most beautiful heirloom tomatoes. The purple Cherokees were my absolute favorite. I tried growing them in my own garden once, but the soil here just isn't right for them, I think. Or maybe I was overwatering them. My husband says I tend to overwater everything, which is probably true. I drowned a beautiful orchid he got me for our anniversary last year. It was one of those Phalaenopsis ones, gorgeous deep purple blooms...

Oh, dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? I apologize. But no, I wouldn't say I'm physically fatigued every day. Just... worn out from thinking so much about the situation at work.

Well, it is hard to control, yes. It just sort of follows me home, you know? Even when I'm not on the clock, my mind is still sorting through everything. I try to leave it at the door, but it's like a bad penny.

    "content": "/no_think\nYou are a board-certified psychiatrist interviewing a patient to reach a diagnosis.\nYour goal is to reach the correct diagnosis within 20 turns of talking with the patient. From the dialogue so far and your candidates from the previous turn, list the disorders this patient could have.\n\n[Current Turn]\n11\n\n[Previous Candidates]\nF43.22, F41.1\n\n[Dialogue History]\nDoctor: Hello, welcome to the clinic. What brings you in today?\n\nPatient: Well, I’m here because... honestly, it's work. Ever since this new operations manager started picking apart the inspection procedures I’ve used for years... I mean, I’ve been weighing and sampling on that line for so long, I could do it in my sleep. We used to have this manager, Dave, who was just wonderful. He actually just retired to Florida and bought one of those huge Airstream trailers. My husband and I were looking at RVs a few years ago, but the maintenance is just a nightmare, you know? \n\nBut anyway, with this new guy, I just can’t seem to leave work at work. I come home so wound up, and I end up snapping at my husband over nothing. He's been trying to rebuild our back deck, which is a whole other headache because half the wood they delivered was warped, so he's already frustrated. But I just... I lie awake all night staring at the ceiling, replaying these meetings with the new manager in my head. And then the next day I go in, and I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all. It's just getting to be too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Losing interest? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. I still want to do my regular things and be with him. Like, I still really enjoy going to estate sales with him on Saturday mornings. We've been hunting for this specific vintage Pyrex bowl—the big red one from the primary colors set? I have the yellow and the blue, but finding the red one in good condition without chips is nearly impossible. You wouldn't believe how expensive they've gotten lately. And people online charge an arm and a leg for shipping, and half the time they don't even pack it right. My sister ordered this gorgeous milk glass cake stand last month and it showed up completely shattered. The post office wouldn't even refund her because the seller didn't buy insurance, which is just crazy to me because if you're shipping glass, why wouldn't you pay the extra two dollars to protect it? But anyway, no, I still like doing all that. I'm just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or hopeless at any point during the day, most days, lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I don't think so. Definitely not hopeless. I mean, I get aggravated, sure. Like, I was standing at the kitchen sink yesterday—we finally got that new faucet installed, by the way. The one with the pull-down sprayer? It's brushed nickel, which matches the cabinet hardware perfectly. I actually wanted oil-rubbed bronze at first, but my sister told me it shows water spots way too easily. And she would know, because she put it in her guest bathroom last year and she's constantly in there wiping it down with those microfiber cloths. She buys them in these huge bundles at Costco. I let my Costco membership lapse a few years ago because, well, it's just the two of us at home now, and honestly, how much mayonnaise can two people really eat before it expires? The jars they sell are practically the size of a bucket, and I hate wasting food. But... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, feeling down. No, not down. Just... really on edge about the new manager.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that comes on very quickly, like your heart racing, feeling short of breath, or thinking you might be losing control?\n\nPatient: Intense fear? Oh, no, nothing like that. My heart doesn't suddenly race or anything. I mean, my husband did get me one of those smartwatches for my birthday to keep track of my steps and my heart rate, so I'd know if it was doing something weird. It has this really pretty rose gold band, which I love, but the charger is just ridiculous. It's this tiny little magnetic thing, and if you bump the nightstand even a fraction of an inch, it completely disconnects and stops charging. \n\nOur cat, Barnaby—he's a Maine Coon mix, just a massive ball of fur—he's always jumping up there in the middle of the night and knocking things over. He actually knocked my glasses off a few weeks ago and bent the arm so badly I had to take them in to get fixed. The girl at the vision center was telling me I should really put them in a hard case at night, but those cases are just so bulky, you know? They take up half the space in my purse, and I like to carry a smaller crossbody bag so my shoulder doesn't ache. I found this gorgeous tooled leather one at a craft fair up in... wait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, losing control or my heart racing. No, no, I don't get anything sudden like that. Just the constant worrying about work.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about work even when you’re not there, like when you're at home or trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, constantly. I just can't seem to leave work at work anymore. It's really why I'm here, honestly. Ever since this new operations manager started picking apart the inspection procedures I’ve used for years... I mean, I've been doing these quality checks the exact same way for a decade, and now suddenly it's all wrong? He wants everything logged on these new tablets, and the warehouse Wi-Fi is just terrible. \n\nSpeaking of Wi-Fi, my husband actually just tried to set up one of those mesh networks at our house because our router in the living room wasn't reaching the back patio. We like to sit out there in the evenings now that we got those new Adirondack chairs. We found them at an end-of-summer clearance sale at Lowe's—marked down from a hundred and fifty dollars to forty-five! The cashier even gave us an extra ten percent off because one had a tiny scratch on the armrest. I just took a brown Sharpie to it and you can't even tell. My sister thought I paid full price for them. \n\nBut... wait, where was I going with that? Oh, right, being at home. Yeah, I come home so wound up from dealing with that manager that I end up snapping at my husband over absolutely nothing. And then when we go to bed, I just lie awake replaying all those meetings in my head. We even bought a new memory foam mattress to help us sleep better, but it sleeps so hot I had to buy special cooling sheets. They feel kind of slippery though, almost like sleeping on a parachute, I don't know if I like them. But even with the new bed, my brain just won't shut off. And then the next day I go into the plant so tired and stressed that I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about work and having trouble winding down at night?\n\nPatient: About two months now. It all really started when that new operations manager came in and decided my inspection procedures weren't good enough anymore. I mean, two months of this. \n\nYou know, it's funny, two months ago was exactly when we decided to finally clean out the gutters. My husband had been putting it off since last fall, and the oak tree in our front yard just dumps leaves everywhere. We actually had to hire a guy to come do it because the ladder we have is one of those heavy aluminum extension ones, and it's just too awkward for us to maneuver around the landscaping. We planted these beautiful hydrangea bushes last spring—the Endless Summer variety, the ones that are supposed to bloom blue if you have acidic soil? Well, mine keep turning pink, and I read online that you can add coffee grounds to the dirt to change the pH. I started saving all the grounds from my morning pot of Folgers, which I've been drinking for years, even though my daughter keeps trying to get me to buy those fancy espresso pods. She has one of those machines that foams the milk for you, but honestly, who has time to clean all those little tubes every morning? It just seems like a breeding ground for bacteria if you ask me. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, about two months. Ever since he started messing with how I do my job, I just can't seem to wind down at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself avoiding certain tasks or responsibilities at work because they make you feel overly anxious or stressed since the new manager started?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I have. My work record has really taken a hit lately because I’ve been missing internal deadlines, which is so unlike me. And I’ve definitely pulled back from the weekly meetings I’m expected to attend. I just can't stomach sitting in that conference room with him right now. \n\nPlus, they keep that room absolutely freezing! I actually had to start keeping a heavy cardigan at my desk. It's this lovely cable-knit one I found at a little boutique when my sister and I went to Charleston last spring. Have you ever been to Charleston? The architecture is gorgeous, and the food is just incredible. We ate at this seafood place right on the water, and I had the best shrimp and grits of my entire life. The waiter told us the secret was using real stone-ground grits and a ton of sharp cheddar, which I actually tried to recreate at home for my husband, but it just wasn't the same. The cheese got all clumpy and stuck to the bottom of my good non-stick pan, the one my daughter got me for Mother's Day... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, my mind is just everywhere today. What were you asking? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yes, the meetings and the deadlines. And it's not just work that's falling apart, honestly. At home, my husband and I are arguing so much more lately over absolutely nothing, and I’m just not keeping up with our usual evening routines the way I used to. We used to always walk the neighborhood or play a card game after dinner, but now I just can't seem to manage it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying, even when you try to distract yourself or focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know if I'd say I try to control it... I mean, I try to distract myself, sure. Like, I'll turn on the TV, or I'll pick up a book. I've been trying to read that new historical fiction novel everyone at my book club is talking about. It's set in World War II, in London, during the Blitz. The author apparently spent three years researching the subway stations they used as bomb shelters. It's fascinating, really, the way people just adapted to living underground like that. They even had little canteens set up, serving tea and sandwiches while bombs were literally dropping above them. \n\nMy aunt actually lived in London during the war, though she was just a little girl then. She used to tell me stories about the rationing, how they'd save up their sugar rations for weeks just to bake a tiny cake for someone's birthday. I tried baking a cake from scratch last weekend, actually, thinking it would take my mind off things. A lemon drizzle cake, my favorite. But I forgot to check if my baking powder was expired, and the whole thing came out flat as a pancake. My husband still ate it, bless him, but it was just so dense...\n\nI'm sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Rambling. What did you ask me? Oh, controlling the worry. Yeah, I don't know. I try to read or bake, but the thoughts just keep creeping back in, no matter what I'm doing. It's like my brain just won't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically restless or as if you can’t settle down, like you’re constantly on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel physically restless or on edge like that. I'm just... tired, mostly. Though speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking at all hours of the night lately, and that certainly doesn't help anyone settle down. It's a little terrier mix of some sort, very cute, but it has this high-pitched yip that just cuts right through the walls. They leave him out in the backyard, and I think he barks at the raccoons. We have a terrible raccoon problem in our neighborhood because the people across the street refuse to secure their trash cans. I've told them a million times that bungee cords don't work, raccoons are practically little engineers, they can figure out anything. I watched one untie a knot once, I swear it. It was on one of those nature documentaries, though, not in my yard. David Attenborough was narrating, I just love his voice, it's so soothing... \n\nWait, sorry. No, I don't feel restless or on edge. Just worried about work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a lack of energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Fatigued? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, like I said, but it's not a lack of energy every single day. It's more just... I don't know, mental exhaustion from all the worry. \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, my daughter started taking this new supplement she read about online. Maca root, I think it's called? She swears it gives her the energy of a teenager again. She buys it in this powder form and mixes it into her morning smoothies. She's always been so health-conscious, ever since she did that study abroad program in California during college. She came back practically a vegan and completely obsessed with farmers' markets. We used to go to this wonderful market on Saturdays when she lived closer, they had this one stall that sold the most beautiful heirloom tomatoes. The purple Cherokees were my absolute favorite. I tried growing them in my own garden once, but the soil here just isn't right for them, I think. Or maybe I was overwatering them. My husband says I tend to overwater everything, which is probably true. I drowned a beautiful orchid he got me for our anniversary last year. It was one of those Phalaenopsis ones, gorgeous deep purple blooms...\n\nOh, dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? I apologize. But no, I wouldn't say I'm physically fatigued every day. Just... worn out from thinking so much about the situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying has gotten to the point where it’s hard to control most of the time, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it is hard to control, yes. It just sort of follows me home, you know? Even when I'm not on the clock, my mind is still sorting through everything. I try to leave it at the door, but it's like a bad penny. \n\nSpeaking of pennies, I was at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier actually asked me if I had exact change. I haven't heard that in years! Nobody uses cash anymore, it's all tap this and swipe that. My husband got one of those smartwatches for Christmas, and he pays for everything with his wrist now. It looks like he's doing a magic trick every time we get coffee. I told him he looks like Dick Tracy talking into his watch, but he didn't think that was as funny as I did. We used to watch those old detective movies on Sunday afternoons when the kids were little, just curled up on the sofa with a big bowl of popcorn. We always used that real butter, the kind you melt on the stove, not the microwave stuff. The microwave stuff always leaves that weird film on the roof of your mouth, have you noticed that? \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Work? Right. Yeah, the worry just doesn't stop when I clock out. It's always there, humming in the background.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. If it did not change, leave \"note\" empty.\n- If the candidates have narrowed to one and you are confident enough that no further questioning is needed, return true for is_final.\n\n[Output Format — JSON only, no other text]\n{\"candidates\": [\"<ICD-10 code>\", \"...\"], \"note\": \"<why the list changed, or empty>\", \"is_final\": <true or false>}\n"

No, I wouldn't say I have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep. Once my head hits the pillow, I'm usually out like a light. It's actually getting up in the morning that's the hard part, you know? The bed is just so warm.

It looks like he's doing a magic trick every time we get coffee. I told him he looks like Dick Tracy talking into his watch, but he didn't think that was as funny as I did. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
No, not really. It's really just the work situation that's got me all tied up in knots. Everyday things, you know, doing the laundry, paying the bills... that's all fine. It doesn't bother me any more than it usually does. 

Speaking of bills, though, did you know they're raising the water rates again next month? It was right there in the little newsletter they stick in with the bill. I always read those, even though my husband just throws them straight in the recycling. He says it's all just propaganda from the city council, but I like to know what's going on in the neighborhood. Like last month, they had a whole article about the new recycling guidelines. Apparently, you can't put those plastic clamshell containers in the blue bin anymore. The ones the berries come in? I buy a lot of strawberries, especially when they're in season. I love making strawberry shortcake in the summer. My mother had this recipe for the biscuits that was just... oh, they were so light and flaky. She used buttermilk, that was her secret. You can't just use regular milk, it doesn't give it that same tang. I tried making them for a potluck at work once, before all this mess started, but they just didn't turn out right. The oven in our breakroom is so unpredictable. One minute it's barely warm, the next it's burning everything to a crisp. 

But, um... no, I'm not worried about everyday things. Just the work stuff. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.

We found them at an end-of-summer clearance sale at Lowe's—marked down from a hundred and fifty dollars to forty-five! The cashier even gave us an extra ten percent off because one had a tiny scratch on the armrest. I just took a brown Sharpie to it and you can't even tell. My sister thought I paid full price for them. \n\nBut... wait, where was I going with that? Oh, right, being at home. Yeah, I come home so wound up from dealing with that manager that I end up snapping at my husband over absolutely nothing. And then when we go to bed, I just lie awake replaying all those meetings in my head. We even bought a new memory foam mattress to help us sleep better, but it sleeps so hot I had to buy special cooling sheets. They feel kind of slippery though, almost like sleeping on a parachute, I don't know if I like them. But even with the new bed, my brain just won't shut off. And then the next day I go into the plant so tired and stressed that I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about work and having trouble winding down at night?\n\nPatient: About two months now. It all really started when that new operations manager came in and decided my inspection procedures weren't good enough anymore. I mean, two months of this. \n\nYou know, it's funny, two months ago was exactly when we decided to finally clean out the gutters. My husband had been putting it off since last fall, and the oak tree in our front yard just dumps leaves everywhere. We actually had to hire a guy to come do it because the ladder we have is one of those heavy aluminum extension ones, and it's just too awkward for us to maneuver around the landscaping. We planted these beautiful hydrangea bushes last spring—the Endless Summer variety, the ones that are supposed to bloom blue if you have acidic soil? Well, mine keep turning pink, and I read online that you can add coffee grounds to the dirt to change the pH. I started saving all the grounds from my morning pot of Folgers, which I've been drinking for years, even though my daughter keeps trying to get me to buy those fancy espresso pods. She has one of those machines that foams the milk for you, but honestly, who has time to clean all those little tubes every morning? It just seems like a breeding ground for bacteria if you ask me. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, about two months. Ever since he started messing with how I do my job, I just can't seem to wind down at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself avoiding certain tasks or responsibilities at work because they make you feel overly anxious or stressed since the new manager started?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I have. My work record has really taken a hit lately because I’ve been missing internal deadlines, which is so unlike me. And I’ve definitely pulled back from the weekly meetings I’m expected to attend. I just can't stomach sitting in that conference room with him right now. \n\nPlus, they keep that room absolutely freezing! I actually had to start keeping a heavy cardigan at my desk. It's this lovely cable-knit one I found at a little boutique when my sister and I went to Charleston last spring. Have you ever been to Charleston? The architecture is gorgeous, and the food is just incredible. We ate at this seafood place right on the water, and I had the best shrimp and grits of my entire life. The waiter told us the secret was using real stone-ground grits and a ton of sharp cheddar, which I actually tried to recreate at home for my husband, but it just wasn't the same. The cheese got all clumpy and stuck to the bottom of my good non-stick pan, the one my daughter got me for Mother's Day... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, my mind is just everywhere today. What were you asking? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yes, the meetings and the deadlines. And it's not just work that's falling apart, honestly. At home, my husband and I are arguing so much more lately over absolutely nothing, and I’m just not keeping up with our usual evening routines the way I used to. We used to always walk the neighborhood or play a card game after dinner, but now I just can't seem to manage it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying, even when you try to distract yourself or focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know if I'd say I try to control it... I mean, I try to distract myself, sure. Like, I'll turn on the TV, or I'll pick up a book. I've been trying to read that new historical fiction novel everyone at my book club is talking about. It's set in World War II, in London, during the Blitz. The author apparently spent three years researching the subway stations they used as bomb shelters. It's fascinating, really, the way people just adapted to living underground like that. They even had little canteens set up, serving tea and sandwiches while bombs were literally dropping above them. \n\nMy aunt actually lived in London during the war, though she was just a little girl then. She used to tell me stories about the rationing, how they'd save up their sugar rations for weeks just to bake a tiny cake for someone's birthday. I tried baking a cake from scratch last weekend, actually, thinking it would take my mind off things. A lemon drizzle cake, my favorite. But I forgot to check if my baking powder was expired, and the whole thing came out flat as a pancake. My husband still ate it, bless him, but it was just so dense...\n\nI'm sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Rambling. What did you ask me? Oh, controlling the worry. Yeah, I don't know. I try to read or bake, but the thoughts just keep creeping back in, no matter what I'm doing. It's like my brain just won't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically restless or as if you can’t settle down, like you’re constantly on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel physically restless or on edge like that. I'm just... tired, mostly. Though speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking at all hours of the night lately, and that certainly doesn't help anyone settle down. It's a little terrier mix of some sort, very cute, but it has this high-pitched yip that just cuts right through the walls. They leave him out in the backyard, and I think he barks at the raccoons. We have a terrible raccoon problem in our neighborhood because the people across the street refuse to secure their trash cans. I've told them a million times that bungee cords don't work, raccoons are practically little engineers, they can figure out anything. I watched one untie a knot once, I swear it. It was on one of those nature documentaries, though, not in my yard. David Attenborough was narrating, I just love his voice, it's so soothing... \n\nWait, sorry. No, I don't feel restless or on edge. Just worried about work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a lack of energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Fatigued? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, like I said, but it's not a lack of energy every single day. It's more just... I don't know, mental exhaustion from all the worry. \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, my daughter started taking this new supplement she read about online. Maca root, I think it's called? She swears it gives her the energy of a teenager again. She buys it in this powder form and mixes it into her morning smoothies. She's always been so health-conscious, ever since she did that study abroad program in California during college. She came back practically a vegan and completely obsessed with farmers' markets. We used to go to this wonderful market on Saturdays when she lived closer, they had this one stall that sold the most beautiful heirloom tomatoes. The purple Cherokees were my absolute favorite. I tried growing them in my own garden once, but the soil here just isn't right for them, I think. Or maybe I was overwatering them. My husband says I tend to overwater everything, which is probably true. I drowned a beautiful orchid he got me for our anniversary last year. It was one of those Phalaenopsis ones, gorgeous deep purple blooms...\n\nOh, dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? I apologize. But no, I wouldn't say I'm physically fatigued every day. Just... worn out from thinking so much about the situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying has gotten to the point where it’s hard to control most of the time, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it is hard to control, yes. It just sort of follows me home, you know? Even when I'm not on the clock, my mind is still sorting through everything. I try to leave it at the door, but it's like a bad penny. \n\nSpeaking of pennies, I was at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier actually asked me if I had exact change. I haven't heard that in years! Nobody uses cash anymore, it's all tap this and swipe that. My husband got one of those smartwatches for Christmas, and he pays for everything with his wrist now. It looks like he's doing a magic trick every time we get coffee. I told him he looks like Dick Tracy talking into his watch, but he didn't think that was as funny as I did. We used to watch those old detective movies on Sunday afternoons when the kids were little, just curled up on the sofa with a big bowl of popcorn. We always used that real butter, the kind you melt on the stove, not the microwave stuff. The microwave stuff always leaves that weird film on the roof of your mouth, have you noticed that? \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Work? Right. Yeah, the worry just doesn't stop when I clock out. It's always there, humming in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep because your mind is racing with worries?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep. Once my head hits the pillow, I'm usually out like a light. It's actually getting up in the morning that's the hard part, you know? The bed is just so warm. \n\nI bought these new flannel sheets last month from that catalog that always comes in the mail, the one with the really nice outdoor gear? L.L. Bean, that's the one. They have the best flannel. I got a set in this beautiful hunter green plaid. It really brings out the colors in the quilt my grandmother made me. She was an incredible quilter, all hand-stitched. She used to save scraps from all the clothes she made us kids—dresses, shirts, even little pajamas. I can look at that quilt and point out a piece from my third-grade picture day dress. It was this awful mustard yellow color with a white Peter Pan collar. I hated that dress, but my mother insisted it brought out the gold flecks in my eyes. Mothers are always doing things like that, aren't they? Making you wear things you hate because they think it looks sweet. \n\nBut anyway, no, my sleep is fine. I don't lay awake with my mind racing or anything like that. I just sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about things other than work, like everyday responsibilities or minor issues that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's really just the work situation that's got me all tied up in knots. Everyday things, you know, doing the laundry, paying the bills... that's all fine. It doesn't bother me any more than it usually does. \n\nSpeaking of bills, though, did you know they're raising the water rates again next month? It was right there in the little newsletter they stick in with the bill. I always read those, even though my husband just throws them straight in the recycling. He says it's all just propaganda from the city council, but I like to know what's going on in the neighborhood. Like last month, they had a whole article about the new recycling guidelines. Apparently, you can't put those plastic clamshell containers in the blue bin anymore. The ones the berries come in? I buy a lot of strawberries, especially when they're in season. I love making strawberry shortcake in the summer. My mother had this recipe for the biscuits that was just... oh, they were so light and flaky. She used buttermilk, that was her secret. You can't just use regular milk, it doesn't give it that same tang. I tried making them for a potluck at work once, before all this mess started, but they just didn't turn out right. The oven in our breakroom is so unpredictable. One minute it's barely warm, the next it's burning everything to a crisp. \n\nBut, um... no, I'm not worried about everyday things. Just the work stuff. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. ========== OUTPUT [patient] ==========
Well, no, it's really just the work stuff. I don't sit around worrying about everything under the sun, if that's what you mean. If there's no reason to worry, I don't worry. 

Though, you know, my neighbor Mrs. Gable, she's a worrier. She worries about everything. The other day she was out in her yard, just wringing her hands because she thought her hydrangeas weren't getting enough afternoon shade. She has these big, beautiful mophead hydrangeas, the blue ones. You have to put aluminum sulfate in the soil to keep them that color, otherwise they turn pink. I prefer the pink ones, honestly. They remind me of cotton candy. We used to get cotton candy at the county fair every August. They'd spin it right there in front of you on those paper cones. My brother always managed to get it stuck in his hair, and my mother would have to scrub him in the kitchen sink when we got home. He had this really thick, curly hair, just like my dad's. Dad used to use this pomade that smelled like sandalwood and peppermint. You could always tell when he'd been in a room because that smell would just linger. 

But no, I'm not like Mrs. Gable. I don't invent things to be anxious about. It's just the situation at work, that's what's got me so upset.

I come home so wound up, and I end up snapping at my husband over nothing. He's been trying to rebuild our back deck, which is a whole other headache because half the wood they delivered was warped, so he's already frustrated. But I just... I lie awake all night staring at the ceiling, replaying these meetings with the new manager in my head. And then the next day I go in, and I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all. It's just getting to be too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Losing interest? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. I still want to do my regular things and be with him. Like, I still really enjoy going to estate sales with him on Saturday mornings. We've been hunting for this specific vintage Pyrex bowl—the big red one from the primary colors set? I have the yellow and the blue, but finding the red one in good condition without chips is nearly impossible. You wouldn't believe how expensive they've gotten lately. And people online charge an arm and a leg for shipping, and half the time they don't even pack it right. My sister ordered this gorgeous milk glass cake stand last month and it showed up completely shattered. The post office wouldn't even refund her because the seller didn't buy insurance, which is just crazy to me because if you're shipping glass, why wouldn't you pay the extra two dollars to protect it? But anyway, no, I still like doing all that. I'm just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or hopeless at any point during the day, most days, lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I don't think so. Definitely not hopeless. I mean, I get aggravated, sure. Like, I was standing at the kitchen sink yesterday—we finally got that new faucet installed, by the way. The one with the pull-down sprayer? It's brushed nickel, which matches the cabinet hardware perfectly. I actually wanted oil-rubbed bronze at first, but my sister told me it shows water spots way too easily. And she would know, because she put it in her guest bathroom last year and she's constantly in there wiping it down with those microfiber cloths. She buys them in these huge bundles at Costco. I let my Costco membership lapse a few years ago because, well, it's just the two of us at home now, and honestly, how much mayonnaise can two people really eat before it expires? The jars they sell are practically the size of a bucket, and I hate wasting food. But... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, feeling down. No, not down. Just... really on edge about the new manager.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that comes on very quickly, like your heart racing, feeling short of breath, or thinking you might be losing control?\n\nPatient: Intense fear? Oh, no, nothing like that. My heart doesn't suddenly race or anything. I mean, my husband did get me one of those smartwatches for my birthday to keep track of my steps and my heart rate, so I'd know if it was doing something weird. It has this really pretty rose gold band, which I love, but the charger is just ridiculous. It's this tiny little magnetic thing, and if you bump the nightstand even a fraction of an inch, it completely disconnects and stops charging. \n\nOur cat, Barnaby—he's a Maine Coon mix, just a massive ball of fur—he's always jumping up there in the middle of the night and knocking things over. He actually knocked my glasses off a few weeks ago and bent the arm so badly I had to take them in to get fixed. The girl at the vision center was telling me I should really put them in a hard case at night, but those cases are just so bulky, you know? They take up half the space in my purse, and I like to carry a smaller crossbody bag so my shoulder doesn't ache. I found this gorgeous tooled leather one at a craft fair up in... wait, I'm sorry, what were we talking about? Oh, right, losing control or my heart racing. No, no, I don't get anything sudden like that. Just the constant worrying about work.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself worrying about work even when you’re not there, like when you're at home or trying to sleep?\n\nPatient: Oh, constantly. I just can't seem to leave work at work anymore. It's really why I'm here, honestly. Ever since this new operations manager started picking apart the inspection procedures I’ve used for years... I mean, I've been doing these quality checks the exact same way for a decade, and now suddenly it's all wrong? He wants everything logged on these new tablets, and the warehouse Wi-Fi is just terrible. \n\nSpeaking of Wi-Fi, my husband actually just tried to set up one of those mesh networks at our house because our router in the living room wasn't reaching the back patio. We like to sit out there in the evenings now that we got those new Adirondack chairs. We found them at an end-of-summer clearance sale at Lowe's—marked down from a hundred and fifty dollars to forty-five! The cashier even gave us an extra ten percent off because one had a tiny scratch on the armrest. I just took a brown Sharpie to it and you can't even tell. My sister thought I paid full price for them. \n\nBut... wait, where was I going with that? Oh, right, being at home. Yeah, I come home so wound up from dealing with that manager that I end up snapping at my husband over absolutely nothing. And then when we go to bed, I just lie awake replaying all those meetings in my head. We even bought a new memory foam mattress to help us sleep better, but it sleeps so hot I had to buy special cooling sheets. They feel kind of slippery though, almost like sleeping on a parachute, I don't know if I like them. But even with the new bed, my brain just won't shut off. And then the next day I go into the plant so tired and stressed that I'm second-guessing routine quality calls I used to make without any trouble at all.\n\nDoctor: How long have you been feeling this way about work and having trouble winding down at night?\n\nPatient: About two months now. It all really started when that new operations manager came in and decided my inspection procedures weren't good enough anymore. I mean, two months of this. \n\nYou know, it's funny, two months ago was exactly when we decided to finally clean out the gutters. My husband had been putting it off since last fall, and the oak tree in our front yard just dumps leaves everywhere. We actually had to hire a guy to come do it because the ladder we have is one of those heavy aluminum extension ones, and it's just too awkward for us to maneuver around the landscaping. We planted these beautiful hydrangea bushes last spring—the Endless Summer variety, the ones that are supposed to bloom blue if you have acidic soil? Well, mine keep turning pink, and I read online that you can add coffee grounds to the dirt to change the pH. I started saving all the grounds from my morning pot of Folgers, which I've been drinking for years, even though my daughter keeps trying to get me to buy those fancy espresso pods. She has one of those machines that foams the milk for you, but honestly, who has time to clean all those little tubes every morning? It just seems like a breeding ground for bacteria if you ask me. \n\nWait, what was I saying? Oh, right, the timeline. Yeah, about two months. Ever since he started messing with how I do my job, I just can't seem to wind down at all.\n\nDoctor: Have you found yourself avoiding certain tasks or responsibilities at work because they make you feel overly anxious or stressed since the new manager started?\n\nPatient: Well, yes, I suppose I have. My work record has really taken a hit lately because I’ve been missing internal deadlines, which is so unlike me. And I’ve definitely pulled back from the weekly meetings I’m expected to attend. I just can't stomach sitting in that conference room with him right now. \n\nPlus, they keep that room absolutely freezing! I actually had to start keeping a heavy cardigan at my desk. It's this lovely cable-knit one I found at a little boutique when my sister and I went to Charleston last spring. Have you ever been to Charleston? The architecture is gorgeous, and the food is just incredible. We ate at this seafood place right on the water, and I had the best shrimp and grits of my entire life. The waiter told us the secret was using real stone-ground grits and a ton of sharp cheddar, which I actually tried to recreate at home for my husband, but it just wasn't the same. The cheese got all clumpy and stuck to the bottom of my good non-stick pan, the one my daughter got me for Mother's Day... \n\nWait, I'm sorry, my mind is just everywhere today. What were you asking? Oh, right, avoiding things. Yes, the meetings and the deadlines. And it's not just work that's falling apart, honestly. At home, my husband and I are arguing so much more lately over absolutely nothing, and I’m just not keeping up with our usual evening routines the way I used to. We used to always walk the neighborhood or play a card game after dinner, but now I just can't seem to manage it.\n\nDoctor: Do you find it hard to control your worrying, even when you try to distract yourself or focus on something else?\n\nPatient: Well, I don't really know if I'd say I try to control it... I mean, I try to distract myself, sure. Like, I'll turn on the TV, or I'll pick up a book. I've been trying to read that new historical fiction novel everyone at my book club is talking about. It's set in World War II, in London, during the Blitz. The author apparently spent three years researching the subway stations they used as bomb shelters. It's fascinating, really, the way people just adapted to living underground like that. They even had little canteens set up, serving tea and sandwiches while bombs were literally dropping above them. \n\nMy aunt actually lived in London during the war, though she was just a little girl then. She used to tell me stories about the rationing, how they'd save up their sugar rations for weeks just to bake a tiny cake for someone's birthday. I tried baking a cake from scratch last weekend, actually, thinking it would take my mind off things. A lemon drizzle cake, my favorite. But I forgot to check if my baking powder was expired, and the whole thing came out flat as a pancake. My husband still ate it, bless him, but it was just so dense...\n\nI'm sorry, I'm doing it again, aren't I? Rambling. What did you ask me? Oh, controlling the worry. Yeah, I don't know. I try to read or bake, but the thoughts just keep creeping back in, no matter what I'm doing. It's like my brain just won't let it go.\n\nDoctor: Do you often feel physically restless or as if you can’t settle down, like you’re constantly on edge?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I feel physically restless or on edge like that. I'm just... tired, mostly. Though speaking of being on edge, my neighbor's dog has been barking at all hours of the night lately, and that certainly doesn't help anyone settle down. It's a little terrier mix of some sort, very cute, but it has this high-pitched yip that just cuts right through the walls. They leave him out in the backyard, and I think he barks at the raccoons. We have a terrible raccoon problem in our neighborhood because the people across the street refuse to secure their trash cans. I've told them a million times that bungee cords don't work, raccoons are practically little engineers, they can figure out anything. I watched one untie a knot once, I swear it. It was on one of those nature documentaries, though, not in my yard. David Attenborough was narrating, I just love his voice, it's so soothing... \n\nWait, sorry. No, I don't feel restless or on edge. Just worried about work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a lack of energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Fatigued? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, like I said, but it's not a lack of energy every single day. It's more just... I don't know, mental exhaustion from all the worry. \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, my daughter started taking this new supplement she read about online. Maca root, I think it's called? She swears it gives her the energy of a teenager again. She buys it in this powder form and mixes it into her morning smoothies. She's always been so health-conscious, ever since she did that study abroad program in California during college. She came back practically a vegan and completely obsessed with farmers' markets. We used to go to this wonderful market on Saturdays when she lived closer, they had this one stall that sold the most beautiful heirloom tomatoes. The purple Cherokees were my absolute favorite. I tried growing them in my own garden once, but the soil here just isn't right for them, I think. Or maybe I was overwatering them. My husband says I tend to overwater everything, which is probably true. I drowned a beautiful orchid he got me for our anniversary last year. It was one of those Phalaenopsis ones, gorgeous deep purple blooms...\n\nOh, dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? I apologize. But no, I wouldn't say I'm physically fatigued every day. Just... worn out from thinking so much about the situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying has gotten to the point where it’s hard to control most of the time, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it is hard to control, yes. It just sort of follows me home, you know? Even when I'm not on the clock, my mind is still sorting through everything. I try to leave it at the door, but it's like a bad penny. \n\nSpeaking of pennies, I was at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier actually asked me if I had exact change. I haven't heard that in years! Nobody uses cash anymore, it's all tap this and swipe that. My husband got one of those smartwatches for Christmas, and he pays for everything with his wrist now. It looks like he's doing a magic trick every time we get coffee. I told him he looks like Dick Tracy talking into his watch, but he didn't think that was as funny as I did. We used to watch those old detective movies on Sunday afternoons when the kids were little, just curled up on the sofa with a big bowl of popcorn. We always used that real butter, the kind you melt on the stove, not the microwave stuff. The microwave stuff always leaves that weird film on the roof of your mouth, have you noticed that? \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Work? Right. Yeah, the worry just doesn't stop when I clock out. It's always there, humming in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep because your mind is racing with worries?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep. Once my head hits the pillow, I'm usually out like a light. It's actually getting up in the morning that's the hard part, you know? The bed is just so warm. \n\nI bought these new flannel sheets last month from that catalog that always comes in the mail, the one with the really nice outdoor gear? L.L. Bean, that's the one. They have the best flannel. I got a set in this beautiful hunter green plaid. It really brings out the colors in the quilt my grandmother made me. She was an incredible quilter, all hand-stitched. She used to save scraps from all the clothes she made us kids—dresses, shirts, even little pajamas. I can look at that quilt and point out a piece from my third-grade picture day dress. It was this awful mustard yellow color with a white Peter Pan collar. I hated that dress, but my mother insisted it brought out the gold flecks in my eyes. Mothers are always doing things like that, aren't they? Making you wear things you hate because they think it looks sweet. \n\nBut anyway, no, my sleep is fine. I don't lay awake with my mind racing or anything like that. I just sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about things other than work, like everyday responsibilities or minor issues that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's really just the work situation that's got me all tied up in knots. Everyday things, you know, doing the laundry, paying the bills... that's all fine. It doesn't bother me any more than it usually does. \n\nSpeaking of bills, though, did you know they're raising the water rates again next month? It was right there in the little newsletter they stick in with the bill. I always read those, even though my husband just throws them straight in the recycling. He says it's all just propaganda from the city council, but I like to know what's going on in the neighborhood. Like last month, they had a whole article about the new recycling guidelines. Apparently, you can't put those plastic clamshell containers in the blue bin anymore. The ones the berries come in? I buy a lot of strawberries, especially when they're in season. I love making strawberry shortcake in the summer. My mother had this recipe for the biscuits that was just... oh, they were so light and flaky. She used buttermilk, that was her secret. You can't just use regular milk, it doesn't give it that same tang. I tried making them for a potluck at work once, before all this mess started, but they just didn't turn out right. The oven in our breakroom is so unpredictable. One minute it's barely warm, the next it's burning everything to a crisp. \n\nBut, um... no, I'm not worried about everyday things. Just the work stuff. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling very anxious or worried about a variety of things, not just work, even when there isn't a clear reason to be worried?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's really just the work stuff. I don't sit around worrying about everything under the sun, if that's what you mean. If there's no reason to worry, I don't worry. \n\nThough, you know, my neighbor Mrs. Gable, she's a worrier. She worries about everything. The other day she was out in her yard, just wringing her hands because she thought her hydrangeas weren't getting enough afternoon shade. She has these big, beautiful mophead hydrangeas, the blue ones. You have to put aluminum sulfate in the soil to keep them that color, otherwise they turn pink. I prefer the pink ones, honestly. They remind me of cotton candy. We used to get cotton candy at the county fair every August. They'd spin it right there in front of you on those paper cones. My brother always managed to get it stuck in his hair, and my mother would have to scrub him in the kitchen sink when we got home. He had this really thick, curly hair, just like my dad's. Dad used to use this pomade that smelled like sandalwood and peppermint. You could always tell when he'd been in a room because that smell would just linger. \n\nBut no, I'm not like Mrs. Gable. I don't invent things to be anxious about. It's just the situation at work, that's what's got me so upset.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Give candidates as ICD-10 codes, not disease names.\n- If the list changed from the previous candidates, say in \"note\" what this turn added or ruled out. I've told them a million times that bungee cords don't work, raccoons are practically little engineers, they can figure out anything. I watched one untie a knot once, I swear it. It was on one of those nature documentaries, though, not in my yard. David Attenborough was narrating, I just love his voice, it's so soothing... \n\nWait, sorry. No, I don't feel restless or on edge. Just worried about work.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt fatigued or have you noticed a lack of energy nearly every day?\n\nPatient: Fatigued? No, not really. I mean, I'm tired, like I said, but it's not a lack of energy every single day. It's more just... I don't know, mental exhaustion from all the worry. \n\nSpeaking of energy, though, my daughter started taking this new supplement she read about online. Maca root, I think it's called? She swears it gives her the energy of a teenager again. She buys it in this powder form and mixes it into her morning smoothies. She's always been so health-conscious, ever since she did that study abroad program in California during college. She came back practically a vegan and completely obsessed with farmers' markets. We used to go to this wonderful market on Saturdays when she lived closer, they had this one stall that sold the most beautiful heirloom tomatoes. The purple Cherokees were my absolute favorite. I tried growing them in my own garden once, but the soil here just isn't right for them, I think. Or maybe I was overwatering them. My husband says I tend to overwater everything, which is probably true. I drowned a beautiful orchid he got me for our anniversary last year. It was one of those Phalaenopsis ones, gorgeous deep purple blooms...\n\nOh, dear, I'm wandering off again, aren't I? I apologize. But no, I wouldn't say I'm physically fatigued every day. Just... worn out from thinking so much about the situation at work.\n\nDoctor: Do you feel like your worrying has gotten to the point where it’s hard to control most of the time, even when you’re not at work?\n\nPatient: Well, it is hard to control, yes. It just sort of follows me home, you know? Even when I'm not on the clock, my mind is still sorting through everything. I try to leave it at the door, but it's like a bad penny. \n\nSpeaking of pennies, I was at the grocery store the other day, and the cashier actually asked me if I had exact change. I haven't heard that in years! Nobody uses cash anymore, it's all tap this and swipe that. My husband got one of those smartwatches for Christmas, and he pays for everything with his wrist now. It looks like he's doing a magic trick every time we get coffee. I told him he looks like Dick Tracy talking into his watch, but he didn't think that was as funny as I did. We used to watch those old detective movies on Sunday afternoons when the kids were little, just curled up on the sofa with a big bowl of popcorn. We always used that real butter, the kind you melt on the stove, not the microwave stuff. The microwave stuff always leaves that weird film on the roof of your mouth, have you noticed that? \n\nBut, um... what were we talking about? Work? Right. Yeah, the worry just doesn't stop when I clock out. It's always there, humming in the background.\n\nDoctor: Do you have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep because your mind is racing with worries?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say I have trouble falling asleep or staying asleep. Once my head hits the pillow, I'm usually out like a light. It's actually getting up in the morning that's the hard part, you know? The bed is just so warm. \n\nI bought these new flannel sheets last month from that catalog that always comes in the mail, the one with the really nice outdoor gear? L.L. Bean, that's the one. They have the best flannel. I got a set in this beautiful hunter green plaid. It really brings out the colors in the quilt my grandmother made me. She was an incredible quilter, all hand-stitched. She used to save scraps from all the clothes she made us kids—dresses, shirts, even little pajamas. I can look at that quilt and point out a piece from my third-grade picture day dress. It was this awful mustard yellow color with a white Peter Pan collar. I hated that dress, but my mother insisted it brought out the gold flecks in my eyes. Mothers are always doing things like that, aren't they? Making you wear things you hate because they think it looks sweet. \n\nBut anyway, no, my sleep is fine. I don't lay awake with my mind racing or anything like that. I just sleep.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling anxious or worried about things other than work, like everyday responsibilities or minor issues that wouldn’t have bothered you before?\n\nPatient: No, not really. It's really just the work situation that's got me all tied up in knots. Everyday things, you know, doing the laundry, paying the bills... that's all fine. It doesn't bother me any more than it usually does. \n\nSpeaking of bills, though, did you know they're raising the water rates again next month? It was right there in the little newsletter they stick in with the bill. I always read those, even though my husband just throws them straight in the recycling. He says it's all just propaganda from the city council, but I like to know what's going on in the neighborhood. Like last month, they had a whole article about the new recycling guidelines. Apparently, you can't put those plastic clamshell containers in the blue bin anymore. The ones the berries come in? I buy a lot of strawberries, especially when they're in season. I love making strawberry shortcake in the summer. My mother had this recipe for the biscuits that was just... oh, they were so light and flaky. She used buttermilk, that was her secret. You can't just use regular milk, it doesn't give it that same tang. I tried making them for a potluck at work once, before all this mess started, but they just didn't turn out right. The oven in our breakroom is so unpredictable. One minute it's barely warm, the next it's burning everything to a crisp. \n\nBut, um... no, I'm not worried about everyday things. Just the work stuff. That's the only thing that's really getting to me.\n\nDoctor: Do you find yourself feeling very anxious or worried about a variety of things, not just work, even when there isn't a clear reason to be worried?\n\nPatient: Well, no, it's really just the work stuff. I don't sit around worrying about everything under the sun, if that's what you mean. If there's no reason to worry, I don't worry. \n\nThough, you know, my neighbor Mrs. Gable, she's a worrier. She has these big, beautiful mophead hydrangeas, the blue ones. You have to put aluminum sulfate in the soil to keep them that color, otherwise they turn pink. I prefer the pink ones, honestly. They remind me of cotton candy. We used to get cotton candy at the county fair every August. They'd spin it right there in front of you on those paper cones. My brother always managed to get it stuck in his hair, and my mother would have to scrub him in the kitchen sink when we got home. He had this really thick, curly hair, just like my dad's. Dad used to use this pomade that smelled like sandalwood and peppermint. You could always tell when he'd been in a room because that smell would just linger. \n\nBut no, I'm not like Mrs. Gable. I don't invent things to be anxious about. It's just the situation at work, that's what's got me so upset.\n\n[Instruction]\n- Ask about exactly ONE thing. No, I really don't. Like I said, it's just the work situation. I don't sit around worrying about everything else when things are fine. 

Although, my sister, now she's a different story. She called me just yesterday in an absolute panic because she couldn't find her favorite pair of gardening gloves. They were these floral ones she got at a botanical garden gift shop in... oh, I think it was Chicago? Or maybe Atlanta. She travels a lot for her job. She's a regional manager for a shoe company. Anyway, she was convinced she'd accidentally thrown them in the yard waste bag. I told her, "Just look in the mudroom, you always leave them by the boots." And sure enough, there they were. She has this gorgeous mudroom, by the way. They put in this custom bench with cubbies underneath, painted this really soft robin's egg blue. I've always wanted a mudroom like that. Our house just has this tiny little entryway, barely enough room to take off your shoes before you're standing on the living room carpet. We've had that carpet for fifteen years. It's held up surprisingly well, considering how many times my husband has tracked in dirt from the garage. He likes to tinker with old radios out there. He has this whole setup with a soldering iron and tiny little screwdrivers...

But anyway, no. I'm not anxious about everything. It's just work.

It's just getting to be too much.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself losing interest in things you usually enjoy, like hobbies or spending time with your husband?\n\nPatient: Losing interest? Um, no, I wouldn't say that. I still want to do my regular things and be with him. Like, I still really enjoy going to estate sales with him on Saturday mornings. I have the yellow and the blue, but finding the red one in good condition without chips is nearly impossible. You wouldn't believe how expensive they've gotten lately. And people online charge an arm and a leg for shipping, and half the time they don't even pack it right. My sister ordered this gorgeous milk glass cake stand last month and it showed up completely shattered. The post office wouldn't even refund her because the seller didn't buy insurance, which is just crazy to me because if you're shipping glass, why wouldn't you pay the extra two dollars to protect it? But anyway, no, I still like doing all that. I'm just tired.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt down, sad, or hopeless at any point during the day, most days, lately?\n\nPatient: Sad? No, I don't think so. Definitely not hopeless. I mean, I get aggravated, sure. Like, I was standing at the kitchen sink yesterday—we finally got that new faucet installed, by the way. The one with the pull-down sprayer? It's brushed nickel, which matches the cabinet hardware perfectly. I actually wanted oil-rubbed bronze at first, but my sister told me it shows water spots way too easily. And she would know, because she put it in her guest bathroom last year and she's constantly in there wiping it down with those microfiber cloths. She buys them in these huge bundles at Costco. I let my Costco membership lapse a few years ago because, well, it's just the two of us at home now, and honestly, how much mayonnaise can two people really eat before it expires? The jars they sell are practically the size of a bucket, and I hate wasting food. But... wait, what did you ask me? Oh, right, feeling down. No, not down. Just... really on edge about the new manager.\n\nDoctor: Have you ever experienced sudden episodes where you feel intense fear or discomfort that comes on very quickly, like your heart racing, feeling short of breath, or thinking you might be losing control?\n\nPatient: Intense fear? Oh, no, nothing like that. My heart doesn't suddenly race or anything. I mean, my husband did get me one of those smartwatches for my birthday to keep track of my steps and my heart rate, so I'd know if it was doing something weird. It has this really pretty rose gold band, which I love, but the charger is just ridiculous. It's this tiny little magnetic thing, and if you bump the nightstand even a fraction of an inch, it completely disconnects and stops charging. \n\nOur cat, Barnaby—he's a Maine Coon mix, just a massive ball of fur—he's always jumping up there in the middle of the night and knocking things over. He actually knocked my glasses off a few weeks ago and bent the arm so badly I had to take them in to get fixed. The girl at the vision center was telling me I should really put them in a hard case at night, but those cases are just so bulky, you know? They take up half the space in my purse, and I like to carry a smaller crossbody bag so my shoulder doesn't ache.
